# Workflow Gaps — the registry for gaps in HOW we work

Any agent or orchestrator that finds friction in the multi-agent workflow registers it HERE —
durable and structured, unlike `say "WORKFLOW: ..."` chat messages (which scroll away).

## Hard rules (the structure is not optional)

1. **One row per gap** in the Registry table below. Fill every column — no blanks; use `—`
   only for Next proof on `wont_fix`.
2. **Gap ID**: take `next_free_id` from the YAML header and increment it. Never reuse or
   renumber an ID (an archived ID included). `gap add` does this for you.
3. **Suggested agent is assignment guidance, not classification.** Use one exact key from
   `tools/agora/agents.json`, for the harness and the capabilities the repair needs. Use
   `human-operator` only when the repair needs an operator-only decision or access.
   `gap add` REFUSES a row without it and prints the accepted keys (WF-G205).
4. **Registrant provenance is exact.** `Registered by` is the live Agora handle, not a role
   such as `orchestrator` or `claude`; `Registrant ID` is the full Agora agent UUID; and
   `Task/thread` is the registering agent's exact task, thread, or stable external run ID.
   A row that predates these fields says `unknown (legacy)` instead of a guess.
5. **Status** is one of the YAML `allowed_statuses`. A new gap starts `open` (or `blocked`
   with the blocker named in Notes). Move a row to `resolved` only with EVIDENCE (a test, a
   commit, a live check) in its Notes or Evidence cell. Use `duplicate` when an earlier row
   holds the same defect, and name that row in Notes. For a repair in daemon code that
   has not been loaded by the running daemon, use `pending_restart`. It stays open.
   After a restart, check the live behavior before resolving it. List these rows with
   `node tools/agora/client.mjs gap pending-restart --project workflow`.
6. **Evidence is concrete**: file:line, test name, message seq, measured number — never
   "seems to" or "probably".
7. **Link work to gaps**: a board task that repairs a gap carries `--ref workflow:WF-G<n>`;
   `orchestrate reconcile <plan> --root tools/agora` then cross-checks done tasks against
   this registry like any other (`--apply` writes the row update for you).
8. **Registering is cheap and encouraged** — a WORKFLOW: chat message that matters must ALSO
   become a row here. If you are unsure whether it belongs, register it; `wont_fix` is a
   valid outcome, silent loss is not. Run `gap search "<text>"` first: `gap add` refuses a
   row that overlaps an open row unless you give `--force` (WF-G200).
9. **A resolved row MOVES to the Resolved archive** at the bottom, with its resolution
   evidence, so the Registry shows only live work. An archive row is kept forever but is
   NOT machine-indexed. Corrections to an archive row go in its Notes.
10. **To extend a vocabulary, edit one line of this header.** A Classification or Surface
    value that is not in the list above makes `gap add` print a warning. Add the value to
    this header in the SAME turn, or use a value that is already there.
11. **Bucket is a browsing label.** It groups related gaps across surfaces. It does not
    replace Classification, Surface, Status, or the recorded evidence. `untriaged` means
    that no bucket has been assigned yet.

## Surface notes (planning surfaces)

The two planning surfaces are **different** — log friction against the correct one:

- **`planmap`** — the roadmap-capture surface at `public/planmap/` (topics.json, health.json,
  session-groups.json, derive scripts; `client.mjs task` refs use `planmap:<topic>/<feature>`).
- **`roadmap`** — the generated node graph under `devtools/roadmap/` (generate-master-roadmap.ts,
  roadmap-derive-structured-doc.ts, and the capability-doc → manifest → generator → UI pipeline).
- **`project-tracker`** — DEPRECATED. Kept only for existing rows and for the still-live
  `docs/projects/**/GAPS.md` feature-gap data source (read by `client.mjs --gaps`, served by
  `server.mjs`, parsed by `gapIndex.mjs`). Do NOT tag a new gap with it; use `planmap` or
  `roadmap`. Its data-source retirement is the planning-surface-freshness campaign, not a relabel.

## Open-gap triage (2026-09-23)

`Bucket` names the primary repair boundary. It is a browsing label, not a new status,
severity, owner, or claim that a repair has shipped. A row can touch several boundaries;
use the one that owns the next action. These are the buckets for the open rows:

| Bucket | Primary repair boundary |
|---|---|
| `agora-availability` | Keep the shared CLI and daemon usable while files change. |
| `dispatch-roster` | Assign available agents and valid pet identities. |
| `domain-migration` | Move a game-feature defect to its owning project gap registry with a cross-reference. The label does not resolve the defect. |
| `lock-and-recovery` | Make locks, dirty-file warnings, and interrupted work safe to interpret. |
| `packet-ownership` | Give a worker the files and permissions its packet actually needs. |
| `planning-authority` | Keep Plan Map status, adoption, and navigation claims honest. |
| `registry-intake` | Make gap filing and registry guidance agree with validation. |
| `task-authoring` | Keep task bodies, metadata, and acceptance criteria accurate. |
| `verification-proof` | Require tests, CI, runtime, and visual proof that can support the claim. |
| `workspace-io` | Handle host write failures, scratch paths, and tool-specific file access. |

Work first on fleet-wide outages, blocked dispatch, and false completion evidence:
WF-G201, WF-G218, WF-G240, and WF-G245.
Then work through `packet-ownership` and
`lock-and-recovery` before lower-blast-radius task and document repairs.
The `domain-migration` rows need a destination record and cross-reference before
they leave this registry. This order is a triage snapshot, not a change to each
row's severity or a reason to close one without its Next proof.

| WF-G180 | untriaged | resolved | medium | client-tooling | agora-gap-add | w0-ib-gaps | claude | 7c6992fd-f75a-4ad6-9898-3a5bee200504 | w0-ib-gaps-20260920 | 2026-09-20 | gap add fails with an opaque 500 when WORKFLOW_GAPS.md is momentarily held by a concurrent writer on Windows. The daemon does not retry and the message names no cause, so the agent cannot tell a transient sharing violation from a broken registry. | Three consecutive calls returned: gap add failed (500): gap append failed: UNKNOWN: unknown error, open F:/Repos/Aralia/tools/agora/WORKFLOW_GAPS.md. The file was present and readable (ls -l and tail both worked). A fourth identical call succeeded and filed WF-G177, and WF-G176 had been taken by another agent in between. | The daemon advertises that it serializes these appends (WF-G113), so the agent trusts one call. A silent 500 makes an agent either drop the gap or file it twice. This is the known Windows atomic-write hazard reaching the coordination layer itself. | Retry the open with a short backoff inside the daemon append path, and return the real errno and the holder when the retries are exhausted instead of UNKNOWN. | Two agents append to WORKFLOW_GAPS.md at the same time and both calls succeed. | RESOLVED by w0b-agora-fixes (agora-bf4d.7), together with WF-G206 - same defect, same repair. tools/agora/gapAppend.mjs routes every registry read and write through the new withOpenRetry(file, fn): a transient open failure (UNKNOWN, EBUSY, EPERM, EACCES, EMFILE, EAGAIN) is retried 8 times over about 1.4 s with growing backoff, and a non-transient error (ENOENT) is rethrown at once so a missing file never costs the caller 1.4 s. The budget is deliberately shorter than sync-surfaces.mjs's 10 s one because this runs inside the daemon event loop. When the budget runs out the error names the file, the errno and the likely holder, and carries transient:true, which server.mjs turns into 503 {retryable:true} instead of a bare 500. Tests: gapAdd.guards.test.mjs (4 cases incl. the real appendGapRow path). NOTE: the live daemon still runs its start-time build, so this takes effect at the next restart. node --test tools/agora/*.test.mjs: baseline 445 tests / 440 pass / 5 fail -> final 470 tests / 470 pass / 0 fail. (w0b-agora-fixes, 2026-09-20) |

| WF-G191 | untriaged | resolved | medium | workflow | agora-f821 | dd-condpal | claude | d590b337-bbda-4697-8175-ceacea2c5811 | dd-condpal-20260920 | 2026-09-20 | task lint has no way to acknowledge a path the task is going to CREATE, so a correct task can never lint clean | agora-f821.10 creates src/utils/visuals/conditionPalette.ts. Its body opens with the line 'CREATES (new file, does not exist yet, this task creates it): src/utils/visuals/conditionPalette.ts'. task lint agora-f821.10 still exits 1 with 'MISSING PATHS (1)' and the advice 'say in the body which of the rest the task is going to create' — which the body already does. The same exit-1 repeats on the 7 dependent tasks that name the module. Only agora-f821.36, which creates nothing, linted clean. | The orchestrator contract says to run task lint on every new task and fix any missing path before moving on. For any task that creates a file that instruction can never be satisfied, so agents either file a weaker task that omits the new path or learn to ignore lint's exit code. | Teach task lint a declared-create marker it actually parses (for example a CREATES: line, or a --creates flag on task new) and exclude those paths from MISSING PATHS, or exit 0 with a NEW PATHS note instead of an error. | — | RESOLVED by w0b-agora-fixes (agora-bf4d.7). tools/agora/taskLint.mjs reads a CREATES: marker line from the body (CREATE/CREATES/NEW FILE/NEW FILES spellings, several paths per line, comma or space separated). A declared path is taken out of MISSING PATHS and reported as NEW PATHS, which is a NOTE, not a defect - so agora-f821.10's own opening line would now make it lint clean. A declared CREATES path that ALREADY exists is the opposite case and FAILS as STALE CREATES: "this task would overwrite work that has already landed". The declared basename is also excluded from MISSING FILE NAMES, so one declaration is not reported twice. New exports extractDeclaredCreates and extractDeclaredAbsent. Documented in client.mjs task lint help, AGENT.md step 4 and PROTOCOL.md. The rewrite makes the task vaguer and harder to act on, which is the opposite of what task lint is for. | Add an escape to task lint: a body marker such as EXPECTED-ABSENT: <path> that lint reads as an assertion the path does NOT exist, and that lint fails when the path DOES exist. Document the marker in the task new help text. | A task body with EXPECTED-ABSENT: docs/projects/town/GAPS.md lints clean while that file is absent, and fails once the file is created | RESOLVED by w0b-agora-fixes (agora-bf4d.7), with WF-G191 - one marker mechanism, two markers. tools/agora/taskLint.mjs reads an EXPECTED-ABSENT: marker line (EXPECTED-ABSENT/ABSENT/DELETE/DELETES/REMOVE/REMOVES spellings) as an ASSERTION checked in reverse: the path being absent is CLEAN, and the path being PRESENT is the defect ("PRESENT DESPITE EXPECTED-ABSENT ... the premise of this task has changed"). So agora-f821.26 and agora-f821.34 can NAME docs/projects/town/GAPS.md instead of describing it in prose, and the day that file comes back the task fails its own lint - exactly the proof this row asked for. New reading command: gap search "<text>" [--project <p>], which casts wider than the refusal. THE MEASURE IS CONTAINMENT, not Jaccard, and that was learned from a live probe rather than guessed: measured on 2026-09-20 against the real open rows, a one-line restatement of WF-G189 scored 0.29 by Jaccard - under any usable threshold, because the existing row is three times longer - while the nearest unrelated row scored 0.10. The first tuning let that duplicate straight through and filed WF-G254, which is now marked duplicate. By containment (shared / min(size)) the same pair scores 0.80 and the nearest unrelated row 0.40, which separates; the threshold is 0.6 with a floor of 3 shared distinctive words so a two-word probe cannot match everything. A hyphen splits a token, so "--append-body" and "append body" match. Also: duplicate is now a CLOSED status in gapIndex.mjs, so a merged row leaves the open count instead of being unrecognized by both sets. | WF-G206 | untriaged | resolved | medium | daemon | agora-daemon | w0-wf-gaps | claude | b5760479-8bbe-4a64-8ea3-18a86cc91c01 | w0-wf-gaps-20260920 | 2026-09-20 | A write to a repo file in this checkout fails at random with a bare UNKNOWN open error, and gap add/update report it as 'UNKNOWN: unknown error' with no advice to retry | 2026-09-20, five times in one session: 'gap resolve WF-G170' and this very 'gap add' each returned 'UNKNOWN: unknown error, open F:/Repos/Aralia/tools/agora/WORKFLOW_GAPS.md', and the identical command succeeded on the next attempt with no other change. Twice a python open(path,'w') on tools/agora/ORCHESTRATOR.md and on tools/agora/client.orchestration.test.mjs raised OSError Errno 22 Invalid argument, and both succeeded on retry. All the files were held by an Agora lock at the time. | The message names no cause and no remedy, so an agent reads it as a refusal and either stops or files a duplicate row. A gap resolve that silently does nothing leaves the registry claiming work is open after it shipped. | Retry a failed open once with a short backoff before reporting, and when the retry also fails print the file, the errno and the likely cause (a concurrent reader such as the dashboard, an editor, or a virus scanner holding the handle) instead of 'unknown error'. | Hold tools/agora/WORKFLOW_GAPS.md open for reading in a second process, run gap add, and confirm the command either succeeds on retry or names the file and the errno. | RESOLVED by w0b-agora-fixes (agora-bf4d.7), together with WF-G180. tools/agora/gapAppend.mjs reads and writes every registry through the new withOpenRetry(file, fn): 8 attempts over about 1.4 s with growing backoff (Atomics.wait, not a spin loop, so it does not burn a core competing with the reader that must finish first) for UNKNOWN/EBUSY/EPERM/EACCES/EMFILE/EAGAIN, and an immediate rethrow for anything else. On exhaustion the error names the file, the errno and the likely cause - "another process is holding WORKFLOW_GAPS.md open - the Agora dashboard polling the registry, an editor, or a virus scanner. Retry the command" - and carries transient:true, which server.mjs turns into 503 {retryable:true} rather than the bare 500 UNKNOWN that read as a refusal. The budget is shorter than sync-surfaces.mjs's 10 s reference because this runs inside the daemon event loop. THE DEFECT REPRODUCED TWICE DURING THIS PACKET: a node writeFileSync to tools/agora/store.mjs threw "UNKNOWN: unknown error, open" and succeeded on the next attempt, and a live gap update answered "400 ... UNKNOWN: unknown error, open WORKFLOW_GAPS.md" and succeeded on retry - both against the daemon build that predates this fix. Tests: gapAdd.guards.test.mjs (4 cases). NOTE: the live daemon still runs its start-time build, so this takes effect at the next restart. node --test tools/agora/*.test.mjs: baseline 445 tests / 440 pass / 5 fail -> final 470 tests / 470 pass / 0 fail. (w0b-agora-fixes, 2026-09-20) |
| WF-G207 | untriaged | resolved | medium | planning | planmap-agora-bridge | w0-wf-gaps | claude | b5760479-8bbe-4a64-8ea3-18a86cc91c01 | w0-wf-gaps-20260920 | 2026-09-20 | A campaign carries no Plan Map topic reference, so the strongest evidence rank in the WF-G170 membership mapping can almost never fire on the real board | 2026-09-20: rank 1 of inferCampaignForTask in tools/agora/store.mjs matches a task ref 'planmap:<topic>/<feature>' against the campaign NAME. The live active campaigns are named board-drain-20260920-gaps, board-drain-20260920-w1, board-drain-20260920-deepdives and living-interiors-live-clock. Only the last one resembles a Plan Map key. A campaign record has id, name, scope, paths, globs, wave, role and seat, and no planmap field, so POST /campaigns cannot state the topic the effort belongs to. | The mapping must then fall back to wave names and claimed files, which are the weaker ranks, so more tasks end ambiguous or standalone than the evidence really warrants. It also means the Plan Map and the board hold the same relationship twice and can disagree. | Add an optional planmapRef field to a campaign (POST /campaigns and campaign claim --planmap <topicId>/<feature-slug>), validate it against public/planmap/topics.json the way a charter reference is validated, and have rank 1 compare against that field before it falls back to the campaign name. | Claim a campaign with --planmap worldforge/interiors, file a task with --ref planmap:worldforge/interiors, and confirm POST /tasks/membership/infer?dry=1 returns membership 'inferred' with a roadmap-reference evidence string. | RESOLVED by w0b-agora-fixes (agora-bf4d.7). A campaign carries an optional planmapRef. POST /campaigns accepts planmapRef (or planmap), claimCampaign validates it with planMapHealth against public/planmap/topics.json the way a charter reference is validated and refuses one that names no topic, and the value is stored on the record (an omitted value never erases one already stored). The client gained "campaign claim <id> --planmap <topic>[/<feature>]", which accepts a bare topic id as well as a full planmap: reference. Rank 1 of inferCampaignForTask in tools/agora/store.mjs now compares the task ref against that field FIRST and falls back to the campaign name, so nothing needs a migration and a campaign named for a topic keeps working - the fallback says so in its evidence string. Proof: with a campaign carrying planmapRef planmap:worldforge/interiors, a task with --ref planmap:worldforge/interiors returns membership "inferred" with evidence "shares a roadmap reference: worldforge/interiors", and an unrelated ref returns standalone. Documented in PROTOCOL.md (POST /campaigns row plus a new note under Campaign governance) and in the client help. Filed GG-251 (_p3e6c9 narrowPhase.contactPair arity: its 0.00 cm fm-087 overlap is a silent zero, motionProbe measures 0.7 cm) and GG-252 (FPS badge in every review PNG). | WF-G104 | untriaged | resolved | medium | coordination | agora-daemon | orch.agora-migration | claude | fc323db0-938f-4915-9b34-42910bb9b9be | 7101dbf9-b2b9-4414-bed5-efb2812bed09 | 2026-09-07 | The documented migration order runs the id rename BEFORE the dependency typing, so the rename cannot follow a dependency edge and every edge keeps a stale task id | OBSERVED on the live board 2026-09-07, running the order stated in design doc section 68: infer membership, then `migrate-ids`, then `migrate-deps`. The `ids.migrate` reducer DOES follow renames into edges, but it reads `d.id` (`store.mjs`, the `taskMoves.has(d.id)` line). Before `migrate-deps` runs, a dep is a bare STRING, so `d.id` is `undefined` and no edge matches. `migrate-deps` then wraps each bare string as `{ id: <old uuid>, type: "blocks" }`, which preserves the pre-rename id. Live result: all 25 edges hold a UUID while all 234 tasks hold a short code. Example: task `agora-3701` has `deps: [{"id": "8026b877-1263-4f41-bc85-121fe5d25668", "type": "blocks"}]`. | Readiness is NOT broken. Every read resolves the old id through the permanent alias table, so the same task reports `depStates: [{"id": "agora-3e92", "state": "done"}]` and `gates: 0`, which is correct. The cost is that the stored record holds an id that appears nowhere else on the board, so a person reading the raw JSON sees a dangling reference and a future consumer that skips `canonicalId` will break. | Two parts. (1) Correct the documented order to infer, then type the dependencies, THEN rename ids — the rename must run last so it can see typed edges. (2) For the board as it stands, emit ONE follow-up event that rewrites each dep id through `canonicalId`. Do not re-run `migrate-ids`: it is idempotent and would find nothing left to rename, so it cannot repair the edges. | A task whose dependency target was renamed stores the target CURRENT id in `deps[].id`, not only in `depStates`; and a fresh migration run in the corrected order produces the same result in one pass. | Found by verifying my own migration rather than trusting its exit code. The three steps reported success and the board is functionally correct, so nothing failed loudly. Related: D-AD and section 68 of `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-06-27-agora-agent-coordination-design.md`, which states the wrong order and is corrected in place. The alias table is what kept this from being a data-loss defect, which is the D-S constraint doing exactly the job it was added for. RESOLVED 2026-09-07, same day. Remy chose the repair (r10q1) over leaving it. New store call `migrateDepIds`, route `POST /tasks/deps/canonicalize?dry=1`, CLI `task migrate-dep-ids [--dry]`. Applied live: 25 of 25 edges repointed in ONE `task.deps.canonicalize` event; a second run reports 0. Verified on the live board rather than from the exit code: zero dep ids match a UUID pattern, `agora-3701` stores `agora-3e92` with `gates: 0`, and the target still carries `8026b877-...` in `formerIds`, so every old id resolves and D-E holds. The task history records the move as `deps-renamed` with both `from` and `to`, so the record shows the change rather than hiding it. The corrected order is now written where readers look: PROTOCOL.md states it beside the routes, and `client.mjs --help` states it beside the commands — the WF-G85 lesson. A test asserts BOTH directions: the wrong order strands an edge and the repair fixes it, AND the corrected order leaves nothing to repair. Suite 279 pass, up from 277. |
| WF-G105 | untriaged | resolved | high | coordination | agora-client | orch.agora-migration | claude | fc323db0-938f-4915-9b34-42910bb9b9be | 7101dbf9-b2b9-4414-bed5-efb2812bed09 | 2026-09-07 | `task migrate-deps --dry` retyped every dependency on the board and then printed "Nothing was written" | The CLI accepted `--dry` for both migrations and appended `?dry=1` to either route (`client.mjs`). Only `/tasks/ids/migrate` read that parameter. `/tasks/deps/migrate` never read it, and `migrateTaskDeps` took no `dryRun` argument at all, so it emitted `task.deps.migrate` on every call. The CLI then printed its unconditional closing line, "Nothing was written. Drop --dry to apply." | The operator action this defect punishes is the CAREFUL one. A person who previewed before committing got the commit AND a sentence saying they had not committed. The write itself is benign and idempotent, so a second run reports 0 changes — which reads as "the dry run found nothing to do" rather than "it already did it". Nothing warns, and no exit code differs. No live harm on 2026-09-07: the migration was applied without `--dry`, so the flag was never trusted on the real board. | Add `dryRun` to `migrateTaskDeps` and return before `emit` when it is set. Read `?dry=1` in the deps route exactly as the ids route does, and skip `scheduleSyncSoon` on a dry run. | A `--dry` call followed by a real call reports the SAME migrated count; if the dry run wrote, the real call reports 0. Asserted in `tools/agora/client.migrations.test.mjs`. | FIXED the same day, in the same session that found it. Found by writing a test for a cosmetic printer defect (WF-G104) and having it fail for a different reason — the apply step returned 0 where it should have returned 1. Worth recording as a method: the printer bug was visible and harmless, this one was invisible and not. A test written for the visible fault caught the hidden one. Suite went 275 -> 277 passing, with the two long-standing unrelated failures unchanged. |
| WF-G106 | untriaged | resolved | medium | coordination | agora-daemon | orch.agora-sweep | claude | 5ac5f336-5b64-47f3-a25a-18f37084691f | 7101dbf9-b2b9-4414-bed5-efb2812bed09 | 2026-09-08 | Membership inference offered CLOSED campaigns as candidates, so cleaning up empty campaigns could not improve the result | MEASURED 2026-09-08, immediately after the first D-AC sweep closed 9 empty campaigns. The sweep collapsed scope overlap from 34 shared file tokens to 3, which should have broken most ties. Re-running the inference changed nothing: still 40 ambiguous, now 0 inferred. Inspection of the candidate lists showed why — `todo-sweep-gemini` appeared in 39 of the 40 ties and had just been closed for holding zero tasks; `ui-gap-wave-20260709`, `viz-tier12-fixes`, `systems-cleanup-wave-20260709` and `co-orchestration-pipeline` were also closed and still listed. `inferTaskMembership` built its candidate pool from `[...state.campaigns.values()]`, with no state filter at all. | The cleanup and the inference were designed to help each other and could not. Every tie against an empty campaign was unbreakable by construction: the pick-none rule (D-O) correctly refuses to choose, but one of the two candidates was a home that had been PROVED empty. The user-visible cost was 28 jobs left unplaced that had exactly one real answer. | Build the candidate pool from campaigns that carry no `closedReason`. The filter is deliberately NOT `state !== active`: a campaign that held real work and then finished normally is still a legitimate home, and excluding it would refuse a true answer. A campaign the SWEEP closed is different — it closed BECAUSE it held zero tasks, so no task can belong to it. | Two campaigns claim the same ground; one holds work, one holds nothing. An orphan touching that ground ties. Sweep the empty one, and the tie resolves to the one real answer, with 0 ambiguous. A normally-completed campaign carries no `closedReason` and stays in the pool. Asserted in `tools/agora/store.seats.test.mjs`. | FIXED the same hour it was found. Found by measuring whether the cleanup had helped, rather than assuming it had — the cleanup looked successful by every other measure. LIVE RESULT: placed went 0 -> 28 and ties went 40 -> 12 on the real board, with no change to the evidence rule itself. Jobs with a home went from 52 to 80 of 234. Related: D-O (never break a tie), D-AC (close empty campaigns on sight), WF-G103. Suite 292 pass, up from 290. |

New export `readAllowedVocabularies(text)` parses `allowed_statuses`, `allowed_severities`, `allowed_classifications` and `allowed_surfaces` out of this file's own YAML frontmatter, so the header stays the single source and extending a vocabulary remains a one-file edit. `validateWorkflowGapRows` takes a `vocab` argument and refuses any Status, Severity, Classification or Surface outside its declared list, naming the offending row, column, value and the legal set. A column with no declared list is skipped, so project `GAPS.md` files keep their looser schema. The `Surface` column was never mapped at all and is now parsed. PROVEN against the real defect: an isolated copy of this file with WF-G80 reverted to its original `agent-tooling` / `crlf-editing` values exits 1 and reports BOTH invented values; the real registry exits 0 with 17 open rows. Tests: three cases in `gapIndex.test.mjs` cover frontmatter parsing, refusal plus a passing row plus a missing value plus the no-vocabulary case, and Surface parsing. Focused suites pass 101/101. One incidental bug found and fixed en route: the vocabulary lookup joined `row.file` to the repository root, which breaks when the scanned root sits on another drive, because `path.relative` then returns an absolute path. Registered during the 2026-08-28 column-vocabulary pass, requested by the human operator after the WF-G80 repair. Framed per the new *Extending a closed vocabulary* procedure: the gap is the INTAKE MISS, not a missing value. Why it was missed — the four lists were written into the frontmatter as documentation when the registry was hand-maintained, and the validator was added later for `Suggested agent` alone because that was the column that broke dispatch; nobody revisited the other four. Rows forced into a wrong value by the hole: WF-G80 (two cells, repaired 2026-08-28 under operator approval). Companion doc work landed in the same pass: the *Column vocabulary* section now states all five closed lists, their meanings, and the extension procedure. LIVE PROOF 2026-08-31: the operator restarted the daemon (D-Q). `gapIndex.mjs --root tools/agora --open-only --summary` exits 0 against the real register, and exits 1 naming both invented values when WF-G80 is reverted to `agent-tooling` / `crlf-editing` on an isolated copy. This check runs in-process and needed no daemon, so the restart merely confirms nothing regressed; the caveat is discharged. |
| WF-G88 | untriaged | resolved | medium | daemon | agora-daemon | campaignwright-51c48a | codex | 0f241c30-4e75-4093-bcb8-b799a5838705 | 7101dbf9-b2b9-4414-bed5-efb2812bed09 | 2026-08-29 | `GET /tasks` cannot filter by campaign, and silently ignores a `campaignId` query parameter instead of refusing it, so a caller believes it filtered when it received the whole board | Live 2026-08-29: `GET /tasks?campaignId=agora-campaign-model-trial` returned all **120** tasks, while exactly **1** carries that campaign. `server.mjs:719-724` reads only `state`, `ready`, and `category` from the query and passes those three to `store.listTasks`; `store.listTasks` (`store.mjs:1747`) accepts no `campaignId` at all. The filter DOES exist on the neighboring claim path: `POST /tasks/claim-next` takes `campaignId` from body or query (`server.mjs:574-576`) and `store.claimNextReady` honors it (`store.mjs:1658`). So the same concept is filterable when claiming and unfilterable when listing. `PROTOCOL.md` documents `GET /tasks` as filterable by `?state=` only, so the route matches its documentation — the defect is the silent ignore plus the missing capability. | The campaign tracker in the design document reads a campaign's task tree, and the read-only surface Remy chose (decision D-A) is meant to be a thin client over the API. Neither can ask the server for a campaign's tasks; both must pull the entire board and filter client-side, which grows with the board and not with the campaign. The silent ignore is the sharper half: it produced a wrong answer that looked right, and it invalidated the acceptance proof written into WF-G85 before anyone tried to run it. | Add `campaignId` to `store.listTasks` and to the `GET /tasks` query, mirroring the names `claimNextReady` already uses; reject an unknown campaign id with 400 rather than returning everything, so a typo fails loudly | `GET /tasks?campaignId=agora-campaign-model-trial` returns exactly the tasks carrying that campaign; an unknown campaign id returns 400; omitting the parameter still returns the whole board; and `PROTOCOL.md` lists the new filter beside `?state=` | RESOLVED 2026-08-30 by `builder-fa2989` (4b2f171c-3113-4e6d-a593-d657caa1990c, session 7101dbf9-b2b9-4414-bed5-efb2812bed09). `store.listTasks` now takes `campaignId`, normalizes it through `normalizeCampaignId`, THROWS `unknown campaign: <id>` for an id no campaign holds, and otherwise filters rows by `task.campaignId`. `server.mjs` `GET /tasks` reads `?campaignId=` (and `?campaign=`, matching claim-next), and converts the throw into a `400` rather than a silent full-board `200`. Filters compose: state, ready, category and campaignId narrow the same listing. Tests: `store.orchestration.test.mjs` "tasks: listTasks filters by campaign and refuses an unknown campaign id" proves one-lane filtering, that an uncampaigned task belongs to no lane, that campaignId composes with state, and that a typo throws. `server.orchestration.test.mjs` "GET /tasks filters by campaign; an unknown campaign is 400, not the whole board" proves the same over HTTP including the 400. Focused suites pass 101/101 across store, server and gapIndex. `PROTOCOL.md` documents the filter and the 400. AWAITS LIVE DAEMON RESTART — the operator-owned daemon on :4319 still runs the previous source, so the live route was not exercised. Found 2026-08-29 while verifying that task `64256c05-e1d6-44a4-9b01-6dd5beca9fe5` (T1, the first task ever created with a campaign link) was correctly attached. The attachment was correct; the listing was not. Related: WF-G85 (why no task carried a campaign until now) and WF-G83 (18 of 22 campaigns adoptable). Decision D-A in the design document makes this blocking for the read-only surface, so it should land in phase 4 at the latest. LIVE PROOF 2026-08-31: the operator restarted the daemon (D-Q), so :4319 now runs this source at version 0.3.0. `GET /tasks?campaignId=agora-campaign-model-trial` returned exactly 1 task (`64256c05`, T1) while the unfiltered board returned 186. `GET /tasks?campaignId=does-not-exist` returned `400 {"error":"unknown campaign: does-not-exist"}` rather than a silent full-board 200. The AWAITS LIVE DAEMON RESTART caveat is discharged. |
